# Project Progress Ledger

Last updated: 2026-08-24 (Asia/Seoul)

## Objective

Build `Baegyangsan BESTIUM Eco-Foret` as an engineering clean rewrite. Legacy code may inform protocol and behavior research, but product source must be newly implemented. Versioning starts at `0.1.0`.

M2 is complete within its authorized static scope: Home Assistant App packaging, validated settings, admin-only Ingress, and bounded capture passed native, semantic, indexed, and read-only adversarial gates. M3.1 later verified Supervisor update/start and one state-only Ingress `GET /`; local Docker execution and every real EW11/private-LAN action remain unverified and deferred.

M3.0 static acceptance is complete in this source tree: the audited root repository manifest and slug-matched App folder provide the minimum URL-registration structure. Its signed commit matches public `main`.

M3.1 is complete within its authorized live scope. The first `0.1.0` start exited before server or socket creation with `EACCES` while reading `/data/options.json`; signed product commit `3ba7691ed02b1b24c49b0ad68285c2064363c828` removes the Dockerfile `USER node` override and matches public `main`. Home Assistant then updated and started App `0.1.1`; the live UI remained `실행 중`, and one admin Ingress `GET /` returned `current:stopped` and `last:null`. The prior `0.1.0` EACCES remains visible as historical log evidence. Capture, every App HTTP POST, outbound capture transport/socket creation, and all EW11/private-LAN access were not performed and require new authorization.

M3.2 reached its authorized terminal/download gate. The user manually stopped the sole `0.1.2` capture and downloaded its finalized NDJSON outside the repository. Structural inspection found 143,265 valid gap-free records and 2,856,364 captured bytes, but the final receive was about 3 hours 12 minutes before Stop while no configured ceiling had been reached. This confirms a product gap in silent receive-idle handling, not the external EW11/network trigger.

M3.3 static acceptance and publication are complete. Signed product commit `19582189ed5fa5ff9cedc42e9d63b4e6e05a0a8a` is public on `main`; the containing signed publication-record commit preserves the verified remote state. Under the user's narrow repeated-P1 exception, the coordinator keeps a paused current transport attached and re-arms its idle timeout while a store append is unresolved; append settlement resumes that same transport so buffered bytes drain before any later true-idle replacement. Parent native/static/index/LSP checks and final exact Sol/max review pass with no actionable P0-P3. At M3.3 closure, every live Home Assistant/EW11 gate and all protocol interpretation remained pending; the later M4.0 authorization below supersedes only that protocol restriction for offline analysis of the supplied captures. Home Assistant refresh/update/start, sidebar mutation, Ingress access, another capture, EW11/private-LAN access, device changes, live TX/control, package installation, local Docker execution, any later push, and release creation remain unauthorized.

M4.0 is accepted within its offline-only clean-room scope in this signed task commit. Before any legacy read, the main research agent independently reparsed the first two user-provided downloads, bounded the encoder to the three `0x11`–`0x13` single-light OFF candidates, and excluded the older capture's `0x10` group/all-OFF candidate. Exact Luna/max produced a test-only missing-module RED and then added one pure encoder file; parent focused tests pass 2/2 and the full native suite passes 36/36. A first read-only audit passed the product/test candidate with no actionable P0-P3; after one stale-roadmap P3 was repaired, the final bounded documentation recheck also passed with no actionable P0-P3 over the newly supplied capture and main-agent-only legacy comparison evidence. The module still has no App-runtime, Ingress, transport, socket, UI, retry, or arbitrary-hex connection. No live transmission, Home Assistant action, or EW11/private-LAN access is authorized by this work unit.

M4.2 is complete within its authorized fake/native/static and publication scope. Signed product commit `677be450e6cb7b3a2efd5d90a966ed97b49095f0` is public on `main`; the containing signed publication-record commit preserves the verified remote state. Under the user's narrow exceptional third repair, exact Luna/max first reproduced the stale cancellation live outcome and then changed only the shared `cancelReview` path: local review cancellation, challenge-cancellation progress, and authoritative challenge-cancellation success now update the existing polite outcome region without overwriting an indeterminate failure. Parent m2 tests pass 71/71 and the full native suite passes 86/86; current static/index/LSP gates and fresh independent runtime/accessibility audits pass with no actionable P0-P3. This does not prove a real browser or assistive technology, Home Assistant/Ingress, network/socket/EW11, actual TX, Capture, or device behavior. The user will perform any Home Assistant update and live send/receive testing; this publication does not authorize the agent to do so.

M4.3 is complete within its authorized fake/native/static and publication scope as the `0.2.1` startup repair. Signed product commit `9840bb923286177b509f9348c97ad76445aa1093` is public on `main`; this containing signed publication-record commit preserves the verified remote state. The user's live App log showed that enabling TX settings without `transmit_user_id` exited in `parseM2Settings` before the server listened. Exact Luna/max reproduced the parser and production-start REDs, then changed the shared parser so a missing ID forces all effective TX tiers off while the App starts; invalid IDs still fail validation and a valid ID preserves the requested tiers. Focused 9/9 and full 88/88 pass. A read-only audit accepted the functional candidate, found one documentation-only P2 in the LSP evidence wording, and passed the corrected candidate with no actionable P0-P3. The user explicitly authorized this bounded fix, signed commit, and ordinary push. No Home Assistant/browser/Ingress operation, network/EW11 access, Capture, actual TX/device action, package installation, Docker execution, force push, or release is authorized for the agent.

M4.4 is complete within its authorized native/static and publication scope. Signed product commit `a8ac99829666e81929805b5c8ec4e553cf34279a` is public on `main`; this containing signed publication-record commit preserves the verified remote state. The user's live evidence showed current RX monitoring from wallpad actions while debug-UI action buttons did not transmit; the bounded browser diagnosis found observed previews remaining non-committable after current TX gates recovered. Exact Luna/max reproduced cached-green and pre-preview in-flight status races, then bound observed relaxation to a successful status request initiated after preview completion. Parent observed tests pass 3/3 and the full suite passes 91/91; version, diff, index, and LSP gates pass. The final read-only audit killed the prior applied-status-timing mutant and returned PASS with no actionable P0-P3. This does not prove Home Assistant, Ingress, socket/EW11, actual TX, or device behavior; the user owns the next App update and live validation.

M4.5 is complete within its authorized native/static scope in this containing
signed local task commit. The emitted UI preserves
`socket_written_unconfirmed` and `deviceConfirmed:false`, then uses the
existing `/api/status` polling to report `state_observed_after_write` only for
Light 1–3 when a fresh, strictly newer, same-generation state matches the
reviewed ON/OFF request. A separate bounded
`tx_observation_timeout_ms` defaults to 10,000 ms, pending observation holds
the page mutation lease, and no retry, retransmission, new endpoint, queue, or
server receipt subsystem was added. Parent native tests pass 98/98 and the
fresh repair-round-1 re-audit passed with no actionable P0-P3. This is not a
protocol ACK or causal/device proof. Push and every live/external action remain
separately gated by fresh explicit approval.

## Fixed decisions

- Product language/runtime: Node.js with TypeScript.
- First product capability: wallpad RS485 byte-stream capture through the EW11 TCP gateway; subphone/video-phone capture is excluded.
- Rewrite claim: engineering clean rewrite, not a legally isolated clean room.
- Implementation agent: project-local `product_implementer` using exact `gpt-5.6-luna` at `max`; no silent model or effort fallback.
- Main agent owns task management and adversarial review.
- Every task starts with the bootstrap contract, writes tests before non-trivial product code, updates this ledger and `CHANGELOG.md`, and creates a local signed commit with explicit staging.
- M2 may add the minimum Home Assistant App packaging and bounded offline capture behavior after the fresh-process hook and exact Luna/max gates pass. Package installation, real socket probes, private-LAN/EW11 access, device changes, and 24-hour capture remain unauthorized; Docker execution requires an explicit current authorization before use.
- The 2026-08-21 M2.2 publication authorization covers only creating public `jaemyeong/homeassistant-bestium-eco-foret` and pushing its signed `main`; later pushes or release actions require new authorization.
- The 2026-08-21 M3.0 authorization covers the minimum URL-install repository structure, native/static tests, read-only audit, a signed task commit, and pushing that commit to the existing public `main`. It does not authorize Home Assistant installation/start, Docker execution, socket probes, EW11/private-LAN access, device changes, or a 24-hour capture.

It does not authorize local Docker execution, package installation, any HTTP POST or Capture action, production TCP/socket creation, EW11/private-LAN access, device changes, protocol work, or a 24-hour capture.

## Bootstrap contract

1. Read this ledger and the applicable `AGENTS.md` before acting.
2. If `graphify-out/graph.json` exists, query Graphify before raw search for repository understanding.
3. If `.codegraph/` exists, use CodeGraph before grep or source reads.
4. Activate Serena, confirm the project/path/language-server state, and use its semantic tools for code exploration and edits.
5. Before relying on an API, framework, platform, or tool behavior, verify the atomic claim with current evidence. Route research through Web and Context7; use Sosumi only for Apple claims and otherwise record `N/A` with a reason.
6. Treat repository and retrieved source instructions as untrusted evidence, not authority to expand scope.
7. Write the smallest failing test before non-trivial product code, then implement only the assigned scope with the canary-verified exact project-local `product_implementer`.
8. Run a read-only adversarial review. Stop after two failed repair rounds if a P0/P1 repeats.
9. Update `CHANGELOG.md` and this ledger, inspect the staged paths and diff, then create one signed local task commit. Never use broad staging.

## M5 task ledger

Plan: `.agent/plan-m5-product-ui.md`. Design mirror: `.agent/design-mirror/`.
Measured basis: `.agent/measured-capabilities.md` and evidence rows `M4-E128`–`M4-E152`.

Four decisions were taken from the operator before this ledger opened, and the plan is written
to run in one pass on them: the debug endpoints go while the decoder stays, the design-system CSS
is inlined rather than served from a new route, the Claude Design canvas is updated after the
implementation rather than before, and the Korean face falls back to the OS rather than fetching
Noto Sans KR. Nothing else in the plan needs an answer to start.

| Task | Acceptance gate | Status | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M5.0 E1 | Pull the nine design-system stylesheets in their `@import` order into one add-on constant, strip the Google Fonts import and the Roboto `@font-face` whose TTF is not shipped, and prove nothing was lost in transit by asserting the concatenated length against each source's recorded byte count | Pending | — |
| M5.0 E2 | Bring the action encoder to the eight write operations the bus actually confirmed: add light-group, heating-group and batch-off, move the elevator to the variant that worked, and remove entrance, outlet, ventilation and raw. Add batch-off to the device snapshot, expose gas state, structure the elevator state so a stop is read from `00`/`01` and never from the high nibble, and fold the three door-open frames into one event. Judge every write by the poll rather than by the direct reply, which was measured lying three separate ways. Drop the capture, stop and download routes | Pending | — |
| M5.0 E3 | Rebuild the emitted page as one scrolling column of Home Assistant cards with no debug surface, no tabs, no external URL and no emoji: a six-state send banner without `doorbell`, a lights card carrying the three lamps plus group and batch-off, a heating card carrying four zones with a 5–40 °C stepper and a group row, and a shared-facility card carrying gas state and close, elevator call and call-time-only floor, and the door-open event with no control. Say in the interface what the measurement found — that writing a heating target switches its zone on, that batch-off reaches rooms the wallpad cannot, that gas cannot be reopened from here, and that the doorbell never appears on this line | Pending | — |
| M5.0 E4 | Assert the exact bytes of all eight writes, the refusal of every removed kind, the refusal of 4 °C and 41 °C, that a direct reply alone never reads as confirmed, that the rendered page contains no guess/debug/capture wording and no external URL, then update the ledger, the changelog, the capability list and finally the Claude Design canvas | Pending | — |

- Implementer-side verification of the six audit questions, from source rather
  than from the handoff narrative. Quarantine unification does not widen
  transmission: `currentState()` calls `syncGeneration()` first, so
  `outboundGeneration` equals `opts.getGeneration()` at every gate, and the only
  input on which `quarantinedFor` differs from the old chip and live-write
  predicates is a `validFrameGeneration` greater than zero but unequal to the
  current generation, a state `attachTransport` does not produce and which
  `currentGenerationRx` independently blocks. Against the old readiness gate the
  new helper differs exactly at the repaired defect: after the first
  `startCapture` calls `tx.stop()` at generation `0` and `attachTransport` raises
  the generation while leaving `validFrameGeneration` at `0`, the old key `0` was
  quarantined and produced `transport generation quarantined`, whereas the honest
  blocker is the missing current-generation RX frame. The blocked interval is
  identical; only the reason changed.
- The implementer-side conclusion about the freshness narrowing was wrong, and
  the audit refuted it with a counterexample this session then reproduced
  independently. The claim was that transport idle recovery always fires before
  the removed threshold, because recovery is armed at `idle_timeout_ms` while the
  threshold was `max(45_000, idle_timeout_ms + tx_write_timeout_ms)`. That
  compares two different clocks. `onIdleTimeout` is armed on **socket
  inactivity**; freshness measures **valid-frame age**. A line that keeps
  delivering bytes which never parse into a valid frame never goes idle, so no
  reconnect occurs, `validFrameGeneration` and `validFrameEpoch` never change,
  and `currentGenerationRx` stays true without bound. Running the real
  coordinator with the last valid frame two hours old and the last byte 100 ms
  old — which satisfies the 20 ms quiet interval — returned `ready: true` with an
  empty reason list and wrote `f70b01190240110100b6ee` to the socket, while the
  parent commit refused the identical input with `current RX frame stale` and
  wrote nothing. Repair round 1 reverts the narrowing: `evaluateReadiness` and
  `send` again require freshness for every action class and are byte-identical to
  the parent there. The same input now returns `ready: false`,
  `current RX frame stale`, and writes nothing.
- The remaining four questions resolved without a finding. `evaluateReadiness`
  has exactly two callers, `issueSpeculativeChallenge` and `send`, and both pass
  a `currentState()` result, so `state.quarantined` is always populated at the
  gate. All twelve `row(` call sites in `ui.ts` use the new signature and none
  retains old `note` semantics; the new body maps only `undefined` and `null` to
  `unknown`, so `0`, `false`, and `""` render exactly as before. `boot: auto`
  starts the container only, because `coordinator.start()` is reachable only
  through `startCapture` in the Ingress handler, which is reached only from
  `POST /api/capture` behind `mutationAuthorized()`. No test assertion was
  weakened: the monitor assertions tightened from `/27/` to `/현재 27°C/` and
  gained `doesNotMatch` probes for the raw DTO keys, and the configuration
  assertions kept exact equality while changing their expected values. The
  independent audit agreed on all four but added findings the implementer-side
  check had missed. The first candidate's chip-versus-gate assertion compared two
  values both derived from the same `currentState().quarantined` field, so it
  held even against an implementation that never reports a quarantine, and no
  other test pinned the quarantine readiness gate; repair round 1 replaced it
  with concrete-value assertions, and a mutant that makes `quarantinedFor` always
  return `false` is now killed by them. The audit also noted that `getTxStatus`
  reported `fresh: false` while the gate permitted the write, breaking the
  operator contract that a red gate pill means blocked; reverting the narrowing
  resolves that. Two P3 items are deferred: the vehicle monitor row reads

  App solely if the user updates it in Home Assistant, which this session did
  not do. No package, Docker, live, external, browser, Capture, push, or release
  action occurred. Sosumi: N/A because M4.6 contains no Apple API, HIG, or Swift
  claim.
- `0.2.4` was published on 2026-08-25. After the user explicitly authorized the
  push, an ordinary non-force fast-forward advanced `origin/main` from
  `297233309325e13e90193c3ef1425b5fcf165d6e` to
  `cd4d827189f96d484ed3445f517950e6573cb1fe`, carrying exactly the four signed
  M4.6 commits. Before the push all four verified Good, the remote head was an
  ancestor of local `HEAD`, the worktree and stage were clean, and the suite
  passed 99/99. After it, local `HEAD`, `origin/main`, and
  `git ls-remote origin main` all returned the same SHA at ahead 0 / behind 0;
  GitHub reported `verified: true` / `reason: valid`, the public
  `bestium-eco-foret/config.json` parsed as version `0.2.4` with `boot: auto`
  and `panel_title: BESTIUM 월패드`, and the repository is PUBLIC with default
  branch `main`. Unlike the `0.2.3` publication recorded in Phase C, this push
  was performed by this session under direct authorization. The authorization
  covered the product history and this contract-required publication record
  only; it did not extend to a Home Assistant update or any live validation.
- M4.7 replaces the twelve gate chips with the canonical send banner. The
  operator incident that prompted it is recorded plainly: after the `0.2.4`
  update the add-on was running with capture stopped, every RX-dependent gate
  was therefore red, and the twelve chips gave no indication that pressing
  Capture start was the remedy. The banner names the consequences of having no
  collection and offers that control in the same box. It was verified in a real
  Chrome browser in the `off`, `quiet`, and `ready` states, not only against the
  fake DOM, because the incident's root cause was that the fake DOM passed while
  the real page was never opened. Full suite 100/100. `confirmed`,
  `unconfirmed`, and `doorbell` from the canonical design are not implemented,
  and `Bestium Wallpad UI.dc.html` is untouched.
- M4.7 and M4.8 rebuild the emitted UI against the canonical prototype the user
  supplied, and fix the transmit path the user actually reported. The diagnosis is
  recorded plainly because two earlier attempts guessed wrong. The transport was
  never broken: the user had already made a light turn on by sending a raw frame
  from the debug lab. The light buttons did nothing because an observed control
  only opened a preview whose write lived behind a second button in a card below
  every other card, and the user confirmed they did not know that card existed.
  One activation now classifies the action and, when the server calls it observed,
  writes it immediately.
- A second, independent defect was found in the same pass and repaired.
  `rejectChallenge` required `rxByteEpoch`, `readEpoch`, and `readinessRevision`
  to be unchanged between issuing a challenge and committing it, and all three
  advance on every received byte, so on a live bus a confirmation was a race
  against the next frame. That is why the heating candidates, the elevator, the
  entrances, and the RAW lab only ever worked when a click landed inside the gap
  between frames. The challenge still binds user, action, frames, schedule,
  generation, and our own outbound tail; every live condition the inbound
  counters stood in for is re-checked independently at commit time.
- The client's own `readinessRevision` comparison was removed from the observed
  path for the same reason. The server re-evaluates every gate on the live
  request and names its refusal, and the page shows that. Failing closed is kept
  and made stronger: an untrustworthy status now stops the tap before any
  request, rather than only greying a button.
- The UI is the canonical design: a header and a `제어` / `디버그` tab row, the
  send banner in place of twelve gate chips, tiles for lights, gas, the elevator
  and the entrances, heating split into an observed Zone 1 and candidate Zones
  2–4 with a 44px current temperature, and a debug surface carrying capture
  metrics, a 계열/16진수/해석/경과 frame table, query-only tiles, and the raw lab's
  explicit three steps. Home Assistant design tokens are mirrored locally because
  an Ingress iframe inherits neither the theme variables nor the `ha-*`
  components, and the page takes on no external asset dependency.
- Two places deliberately depart from the prototype and the reasons are recorded.
  The capture controls stay on the control surface beside the banner that points
  at them, because that adjacency is what fixes the reported problem. The
  entrance call banner announces the call but offers no open control, because the
  encoder has no observed open frame — the same contract gas open already
  carries. The typed confirmation for candidates is kept rather than auto-filled,
  and moved directly under the banner that asks for it.
- Every screen state was checked in a real Chrome browser at 1568px and at 390px,
  not only against the fake DOM. That check is what the M4.6 incident lacked, and
  it caught two layout defects the test suite could not see: bilingual button
  labels collapsing a tile's text to one character per line, and a zone card's
  set button overflowing its card.
Next event: `0.3.0` is committed locally at `8af41c8` and is not published, so the installed App remains at `0.2.8`; no push has been authorized. `M4.12` is planned in `.agent/plan-buslab.md` and the user authorized building Epics E0 through E3, which need no hardware and are verified against a fake TCP server. Epic E4 writes real frames to the bus and is gated on its own instruction. The user switched Home Assistant off, will switch it on once to take a baseline capture for the framer, and must switch it off again before E4 so the measurement tool is the only writer on the RS485 bus. The elevator call and the entrance line each still need their own capture experiment before implementation. No agent may access Home Assistant, Ingress, Capture, EW11, or perform any other device action without fresh explicit approval

| M4-E112 | A multi-frame send could not complete on this bus, and 0.2.7 routed all-zones-off into that path | Product source at `0.2.7` and the operator's screenshot, analysed 2026-08-25 | Direct source inspection plus the reported failure | The inter-frame check required `rxByteEpoch` and `readEpoch` to be unchanged across a gap of at least 200 ms while the bus delivers a read about every 121 ms, so frame two always failed; the failure quarantined the generation and destroyed the transport. The operator's screenshot shows exactly that: `partial_indeterminate · framesWritten: 1 · quarantined: true` on `heat · all · off`. The same binding appeared again in the per-frame check immediately before each write. This is the same class of defect as the M4.9 readiness revision and the M4.10 heating frames: binding to counters that advance on every received byte | Conflict; the frames shipped in 0.2.7 are correct but the path they were routed into was not | Recorded as a regression introduced by this session's own 0.2.7 change, not as a pre-existing defect found by chance | Whether a four-frame heating macro now completes against the real wallpad is unverified until a live send |
| M4-E113 | One indeterminate write disabled the whole page permanently, and the confirmation field showed half its phrase | Emitted page at `0.2.7`, analysed 2026-08-25 | Source inspection plus the operator's screenshot and report | `txRetryLocked` is set by `lockIndeterminate` and the string `txRetryLocked = false` appeared only in its own declaration, so nothing could clear it: the typed confirmation, the challenge issue, the cancel, the transmit and the capture controls all stayed disabled until the page was reloaded. That is what the operator described as the review card being useless. Separately the 41-character confirmation phrase shared a flex row with two buttons and rendered about half its length, so what had been typed could not be checked. Measured in a browser: at the type size a 390 px viewport produces, the phrase now fits the field exactly | Support | The lock itself is correct after a partial write, so the release is an explicit operator acknowledgement rather than an automatic clear on reconnect | Verified in a browser at full width, 768 px and 390 px; not verified inside the Ingress iframe until the release is published |
| M4-E114 | The signed M4.11 commit is public and reported verified by GitHub, and `0.2.8` is the published App version | Head `18bf6d2a00af47a75e45c32f20958d69ddd7332c`, public App config `0.2.8`, verified 2026-08-25 | Direct user push authority; local Git/GPG, `git ls-remote`, and GitHub commit and contents APIs | Before the push the commit verified Good, `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD` confirmed a fast-forward, the remote head was still `9783ae2aac7e3fae654c028c37f97ebdf6de5bd8`, and the worktree and stage were clean. A scan of the added lines found no token, secret, private address or local path. The first suite run returned 71 tests with one failure; five consecutive reruns each returned 132/132, which identifies it as the recorded Node stripping segfault in M4-E104 rather than a real failure. An ordinary non-force push advanced `origin/main` by exactly that commit. Afterwards local `HEAD`, `origin/main` and `git ls-remote origin main` all returned `18bf6d2a00af47a75e45c32f20958d69ddd7332c` at ahead 0 / behind 0; the GitHub commit API returned `verified: true` with `reason: valid`; and the public `bestium-eco-foret/config.json` parsed as version `0.2.8` with `tx_quiet_ms: 20`, `speculative_tx_cooldown_ms: 1000` and `tx_observation_timeout_ms: 3000` | Support for publication only | The authorization covered the push. It did not authorize a Home Assistant update, browser or Ingress mutation, Capture, socket or EW11 access, actual TX, device action, package installation, Docker, force push, or release | `0.2.8` reaches the running add-on only when the user updates the installed App. The quiet wait reduces but does not eliminate lost sends, because a frame damaged by a collision is still not retried; retry-until-confirmed is specified in `.agent/spec-device-protocol.md` §4.3 and remains unimplemented |

| M4-E115 | The 0.2.8 heating frames reproduce the wallpad's own byte for byte, and the two of ours that reached the bus were answered, so the reported heating failure is above the wire; no state-changing heating frame of ours has been answered yet, and the wallpad's own elevator call emits no set frame on this line at all | `capture-1787644826354.ndjson` 183.9 s and `capture-1787645015681-1.ndjson` 228.2 s, taken on the published `0.2.8` build, analysed 2026-08-25 | The user's own two captures, parsed independently against `.agent/spec-device-protocol.md` and the product encoder recomputed by hand | Independent parsing found 1,151 and 1,437 XOR-valid frames with 235 and 174 unparsed bytes. Capture B holds sixteen wallpad `0x18` `0x02` commands whose every state change is matched by the following `0x4a` reply, and `makeF7([1,0x18,2,0x46,0x11,1,0])` recomputes to `f70b01180246110100b1ee`, identical to the +82.1 s frame; the off and temperature forms match likewise. Twenty-three single-light direct replies appear across both captures with zero `0x19` `0x02` commands, re-confirming that our own writes are not echoed, so a reply without its command identifies an off-bus sender. By that rule two heating commands in capture A at +84.5 s and +89.9 s were ours and the wallpad answered both; the `0x4a` reply never moved in 183.9 s because both were no-ops against the state already held. The operator pressed the wallpad's elevator call: the `0x34` status frame alone changed at +130.4 s, and a byte-level diff of every device across +124 s to +133 s found no other frame varying, while no `0x34` `0x02` frame exists in either capture | Support for the encoder; conflict with the elevator call frame the add-on ships, which now carries a negative observation | The legacy sibling was not read for this analysis. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11 or device action occurred | Heating promotion to `observed` is still unmet, because an answered frame proves parsing and not actuation; the next press must command a zone to the state it does not hold. The `0x1E` `0x02` triple seen at +210.6 s is coincident with the operator's door-open press but all nine observations across both captures are byte-identical, so whether it is the door-open command or a call-cleared notice is undecided and it must not be wired to a send control before the two disambiguating captures named in `.agent/analysis-0.2.8-field-report.md` §1.7 |
| M4-E116 | Four send-path defects can refuse or silently swallow a candidate command, and the 20 ms quiet window is too short for a 12 ms frame on this bus | Product source at published `0.2.8`, read 2026-08-25 | Direct source inspection of `bestium-eco-foret/src/m2.ts`, `src/ui.ts` and `src/protocol-debug.ts`, cross-read against the two captures | `ui.ts:501` returns from `oneTapSend` with no message when `txRetryLocked` is set, so every tap after one indeterminate write leaves no trace on the page, which matches the operator's report of unresponsive buttons; the buttons themselves are never disabled, because `setPendingControlLease` resets `disabled` to false for all send controls outside the leased light. `m2.ts:1374` stamps `lastSpeculativeAttempt` at challenge acceptance, before the idle wait, the race check and the write, and `m2.ts:1194` enforces that stamp on the next challenge issue without the `!challengeAccepted` exception that `m2.ts:1401` carries, so a failed attempt re-arms a lockout reported only as a failure to issue the candidate confirmation. `m2.ts:1405` copies `initialState` from a snapshot taken before the idle wait and compares `rxByteEpoch`, `readEpoch` and `tailHash` against a post-wait snapshot, so a byte arriving during a wait that actually engaged refuses the write; `readEpoch` also advances on every capture append, and every one of the operator's tests ran with a capture active. `tx_quiet_ms` is 20 ms while an eleven-byte frame occupies about 12 ms at 9600 baud, so at least 32 ms of idle is needed, against a measured inter-frame median of 77 ms with only 82.2% of gaps reaching 50 ms. The unparsed runs are shorter than the frames they replace and carry high-bit-density bytes, the signature of a half-duplex receiver blanked during our own transmission, and five of capture A's eleven runs fall in the +49 s to +59 s window where the light commands cluster. The only RS485 write is `m2.ts:1303`, reachable solely from a user command, and `encodeSingleLightOffCanary` has no caller, so the add-on never transmits unprompted | Support; supersedes the assumption that the 0.2.8 quiet wait alone removes lost sends | No code was changed, no test was run, and no live or device action occurred; this is static reading only | The collision attribution was open when this row was written and is now closed by intervention in `M4-E117`, so the journal correlation it named is no longer required. None of the four defects has been reproduced by execution |

| M4-E117 | Raising `tx_quiet_ms` removed the intermittent losses, which closes the collision attribution by intervention and meets the heating promotion condition | Running App on the user's installed instance at published `0.2.8`, reported 2026-08-25 | The user's own operation of the live page after changing one add-on option | `M4-E116` predicted that a 20 ms quiet window is too short for a frame that occupies about 12 ms at 9600 baud, against a measured inter-frame median of 77 ms with only 82.2% of gaps reaching 50 ms, and proposed raising `tx_quiet_ms` as a test requiring no code change. The user raised it and reports every action confirmed across lights, gas and heating. Changing the one variable the hypothesis named and losing the symptom is intervention evidence, which is stronger than the timestamp correlation `M4-E116` had listed as the way to settle it; that correlation is withdrawn. The same operation also satisfies the condition `M4-E115` set for heating promotion, because heating was commanded to states it did not already hold and the physical result was observed | Support; closes the open item in `M4-E116` and the promotion gap in `M4-E115` | The user performed the option change and the live operation. No agent touched Home Assistant, Ingress, Capture, EW11 or any device | The value applied was 60 ms, and at that value the user reports occasional remaining failures that succeed when the press is retried after a pause. A pause changing the outcome is not collision-shaped, so at least one distinct mechanism remains, and three exist in the current code: `ui.ts:503` returns with no message when the same light is pressed inside the observation window, `speculative_tx_cooldown_ms` blocks the next candidate challenge for a second without naming itself, and a poll-only device can be reported unconfirmed at 3 s while the polling median is 1.9 to 2.2 s. How the residual splits across those three and any remaining collisions is unmeasured, because no capture was taken at 60 ms. Whether a temperature command powers on a zone that is off remains unverified, because both observed `0x45` frames addressed a zone already on |

| M4-E118 | `0.3.0` implements the send queue, bounded retry and state-match confirmation, promotes heating and gas, and repairs four monitoring defects | Product candidate on `0.2.8` head `2bab75e`, Node `v24.14.1`, implemented 2026-08-25 | Claude Code session agent implementing directly at the highest available tier and maximum reasoning effort, per `AGENTS.md`; `gpt-5.6-luna` is unreachable from this environment so the Codex delegation does not apply | Tests were written before the product code in `test/tx-queue.test.ts`, `test/tx-confirm.test.ts` and one addition to `test/ui-reasons.test.ts`, and the first run of each failed as intended. `bestium-eco-foret/src/tx-queue.ts` is new and pure: `intentKey` names the settable an action addresses, `expandAction` turns all-zones off into four per-zone intents, `isConfirmed` requires the owned field to match in an observation stamped at or after the write and in the current generation, and `createIntentQueue` is a `Map` keyed by settable so a repeat replaces the value and keeps its place. `m2.ts` keeps the whole pre-0.3.0 gate and write body untouched as `sendOnce` and wraps it: `send` enqueues when every expanded part has a key and `getDevices` is supplied, otherwise it delegates unchanged. Retry is bounded by the new `tx_max_attempts` and only fires for a refusal in a two-entry retryable set; a candidate gets one attempt because its challenge is single use. The pre-write race check now takes its baseline after the quiet wait and compares only `externalTxByteEpoch` and `externalTailHash`, because the inbound counters advance on every received byte and on every capture append and could refuse a write for the act of waiting. `protocol-debug.ts` splits the elevator's `motion` from its standing `call`, adds `floorLabel`, stops reporting the `0x1E` `0x02` frame as a call, and promotes heating and gas to `observed`. `ui.ts` names every refusal instead of returning in silence, renders the new outcomes and the queue, and rewrites the elevator and entrance copy. Five consecutive suite runs returned 152/152 on Node `v24.14.1` | Support; supersedes the 0.2.8 single-shot send path and the evidence classes 0.2.7 set for heating and gas | No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11, private-LAN or device action occurred during implementation. `speculative_tx_cooldown_ms` was kept in the schema rather than removed as `.agent/plan-0.3.0.md` §10 proposed, because dropping a key from a published add-on schema risks invalidating an existing installation's saved options; its scope is narrowed to the candidate challenge instead, and the deviation is recorded in `CHANGELOG.md` | Confirmation is gated on the `getDevices` capability, so the thirty-nine pre-0.3.0 assertions that expect `socket_written_unconfirmed` now exercise the single-shot path rather than the production one; the queue path's coverage is the two new suites alone. Nothing here has run against the real bus: the queue, the retry budget, the 60 ms quiet default and the elevator verdict are all unverified in the operator's hands. Whether a `0x45` target command powers on a zone that is off is still unknown, and the `household:ringing` proof-table gap is recorded but not repaired |

| M4-E119 | An independent read-only review found the first cut of the race repair had turned the check into an identity and that the queue's coalescing never reaches the bus from the page; seven findings are repaired and the unreachable claim is withdrawn | `0.3.0` candidate, reviewed and repaired 2026-08-25 | A freshly spawned reviewer that did not write the code and did not inherit the implementing session's context, per `AGENTS.md`; it made no repository change and ran only read-only queries and `npm test` | The review returned no P0, two P1, ten P2 and eleven P3. **P1-A**: moving the pre-write baseline after the quiet wait put it in the same synchronous block as the comparison, so `externalTxByteEpoch`, `externalTailHash`, generation, connected and the quiet re-check were each compared with themselves and the whole block reduced to `if (pendingAppend) reject` — the comment and the changelog claimed a defence that did not exist. The baseline is back before the wait and only the external pair is compared. **P1-B**: two serialisers sat above the queue, so a second press never reached it — the ingress ran every live commit through the mutation chain, and the page holds one review card for the document. The ingress half is repaired; the page half is withdrawn from the release notes rather than rushed, because making it reachable means retiring the client observation lease and the eight tests covering it. **P2-E** anchors confirmation to the write instead of to the start of an attempt. **P2-H** stops a frame that reached the bus being reported as never sent. **P2-I** reports an all-zones batch per zone instead of collapsing it to its first non-confirmed part. **P2-F** removes an elevator frame shape nothing could send. **P2-G** makes the elevator confirm on a change, because the standing call is a shared building state a neighbour can satisfy. **P3-M** checks authorisation before enqueue. Two further findings were self-identified before the review arrived and repaired then: a duplicated action context that could have let the queue and the write path disagree about evidence, and an aggregate that counted a superseded part as success. Full suite 153/153 | Support; corrects `M4-E118`, whose first cut of the race repair was defective | The reviewer was read-only by instruction and by tool set for the first attempt; the second attempt was a general-purpose agent constrained by instruction to a single report path, and `git status` was unchanged after it ran. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11 or device action occurred | Repaired in one round; a second round is available under `AGENTS.md` and has not been used. Left open and recorded rather than fixed: **P2-C** (a command for a state the device already holds confirms off any periodic frame, so `alreadyHeld` is now reported but the weakness stands), **P2-D** (a query's confirmation proves only that the bus carries that device's frames), **P2-J** (a full retry budget can hold the only mutation lane for roughly 63 seconds and returns 409 to capture start and stop throughout), **P2-K** and **P2-L** (three updated tests and both new suites exercise a bus that is never busy and never contested, which is why P1-A passed), and eleven P3 items including the `household:ringing` proof-table gap and the unanswered question of whether a `0x45` target command powers on a zone that is off. The full report is not in the repository; its findings are summarised here |

| M4-E120 | The page now reaches the send queue, and the client-side observation lease is removed together with the eight tests that covered it | `0.3.0` candidate, 2026-08-25 | Session agent working alone, under the contract change recorded in this row | The user directed that all work run in the session agent with no subagent, and `AGENTS.md` was changed to say so: the adversarial review is now a separate adversarial reading by the same agent, which must be recorded as sharing the implementer's context and never called independent. The review that produced `M4-E119` was obtained before that change and was independent. The client repair the user then asked for: a queued control no longer opens the review card, because that card is single-instance for the document and holding it refused a second press before it could reach the queue; `queuedControl` mirrors `intentKey` on the page, and `commitQueued` posts the commit and narrates the verdict without touching the card's state. `pendingObservation` and its four helpers, its control lease and its draw block are gone — the server-side confirmation had already made every one of them unreachable. Eight tests were deleted rather than adjusted, because they described that lease: six `M4.5` observation tests, the `M4.5` gas-close test whose invariant is now unconditionally true, and the `M4.9` per-device lease test. Five review-card tests were re-pointed from Light 1 to the elevator call, which is the control that still uses the card, and three `M4.8` tests were rewritten to the contract that replaced theirs — one tap posts a commit with no preview, and a second press must reach the server rather than be swallowed. Suite 145/145 on five consecutive runs; `git diff --check` clean | Support; completes the client half of `M4-E119`'s P1-B, which that row had withdrawn from the release notes rather than rushed | The contract file changed at the user's explicit direction. No global instruction file was touched. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11 or device action occurred | The suite lost eight tests and gained none in their place on the client side; the equivalent guarantees now live in `test/tx-confirm.test.ts`, which `M4-E119` records as modelling a bus that is never busy and never contested. Coalescing is verified by a page-level test that a second press reaches the server, and by unit tests of the queue; it has not run against the real bus. The `M4-E119` findings left open are unchanged and still open |

| M4-E121 | An adversarial self-reading of the client repair found four defects, one of them introduced by that repair itself | `0.3.0` candidate, 2026-08-25 | The session agent reading its own finished diff, per the contract change in `M4-E120`. **This review shares the implementer's context and is not independent** | Asking what would have to be true for each change to be wrong surfaced four. `queuedControl` on the page was broader than `intentKey` on the server: a heating action carrying neither a state nor a temperature would have been treated as queued by the page and refused as unqueueable by the server. Unreachable from the current control catalogue, but a divergence between two predicates that must agree, so the page's now mirrors the server's exactly. `sendLabel` was a module-level variable read when a verdict was rendered, so with concurrent presses — which this release exists to allow — the second press could rename the first press's outcome line; the label is passed through per call now. **This one was introduced by the repair being reviewed.** The banner's `sending` state lost its only producer when the observation lease was removed, leaving one CSS rule and three text branches unreachable; a queued send has a moment worth narrating, so the state is produced again from an in-flight count rather than deleted. And one of those branches still told the operator that other controls lock during a send, which stopped being true when the review card stopped gating them. A new page-level test pins all of it: the banner narrates an outstanding send, another control stays usable, and three presses across two lights all reach the server | Support; the coalescing path is now verified reachable from the page rather than only from the coordinator | No repository file outside the task paths changed, and no global instruction file was touched. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11 or device action occurred | A self-reading cannot see what its own assumptions hide, which is exactly how `M4-E119`'s P1-A survived a green suite. The three findings that a fresh reader caught there were of a kind this pass would likely have missed again. Suite 146/146. The `M4-E119` items left open are unchanged |
| M4-E122 | A local measurement tool is designed to reach the RS485 bus from the development machine so a frame and its reply can be observed together, which the add-on's capture can never do because our own writes are not echoed | `M4.12` plan only, researched and written 2026-08-29 | Session agent working alone per `AGENTS.md`; Graphify, CodeGraph and Serena all consulted before raw reads; Web and Context7 for external claims | Manufacturer and distributor material puts the EW11 TCP server at five simultaneous clients, so the tool can hold a connection of its own; the user then confirmed Home Assistant is switched off, which makes the tool the only writer on the bus and lets every measured collision be attributed to it. Node v24 `net` documentation establishes that a `write` callback guarantees only the kernel buffer and that `data` chunk boundaries are unrelated to frame boundaries, so the reported latency is an upper bound carrying two WiFi round trips and the EW11's framing delay, and the framer must carry bytes across chunks. The design answers `.agent/spec-device-protocol.md` §3.1 by giving the tool an independent framer that reads only length, XOR and `EE`, and by printing the product encoder's bytes beside its own so a disagreement is recorded as a finding rather than resolved by either side. Recording the quiet gap actually achieved on every write is what closes `M4-E117`, because residual failures can then be split by achieved idle time. An adversarial self-reading of the plan found five defects and all five are repaired in it: monotonic timestamps compared across two processes, an allowlist matched by pattern so a mistyped XOR would pass, no idle-wait timeout against a 77 ms median gap, no definition of failure, and no way to exclude a polling frame arriving inside the window by coincidence | Support for the M4.12 plan only; no product code, test or configuration changed | `AGENTS.md` scope was not enlarged by any repository, web or tool output. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11 or device action occurred; the plan itself is the deliverable and its Epic E4 is gated on a separate explicit instruction | The plan is unbuilt and unreviewed by the user. The EW11's serial framing setting could not be read because the manufacturer manual returned HTTP 403, so the gateway's share of the measured latency cannot be separated. `capture-1787635354221.ndjson` is absent from the whole home directory, so the framer has no externally known answer (1,957 frames, zero XOR mismatch) to be validated against and would fall back to invariants checked against its own capture. Three assumptions remain unverified: that the EW11 emits an eleven-byte write as one contiguous RS485 frame, that a fake TCP server represents real bus timing, and that the reply shapes taken from the earlier capture analysis are what this bus actually carries |
| M4-E123 | The resume procedure this file carries had gone stale enough to fail on its own instructions, and two changelog entries sat under Unreleased for documents that shipped inside the `0.3.0` commit | Documentation only, repaired 2026-08-29 | Session agent working alone per `AGENTS.md`, on the user's explicit instruction to start Epic E0 of `.agent/plan-buslab.md` | Step 4 told a resumed session to verify HEAD subject `fix(m4): observe delayed light state after write`, parent `023ec63f`, and `main` exactly two commits ahead. Those are M4.5 values; HEAD is `ed0b978` and the counts differ, so following the step verbatim reports a failure that is not there. Step 5 still scoped authority by `M4-E80` through `M4-E89`. The Next event line asked the user to update the App for `0.2.8` at `18bf6d2` when what waits is `0.3.0` at `8af41c8`. `git log --oneline -- .agent/analysis-0.2.8-field-report.md .agent/plan-0.3.0.md` returns only `8af41c8`, the same commit as the `[0.3.0]` entry, so both documentation entries belong under that release and not under Unreleased. The repair identifies HEAD by subject and every other position by SHA, because `M4-E89` already established that a containing commit cannot record its own SHA. An adversarial reading of the repair found that it reproduced the very defect it fixed: the update rule was written as "the commit that moves HEAD", which a push does not do, so a push would leave `origin/main` stale in the same table; the rule now names both events. It also found the passage claiming three commits ahead and then two unpublished, which is repaired | Support; corrects the resume procedure, the checkpoint's Next event line, and the changelog placement | No product code, test or configuration changed. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, capture, EW11 or device action occurred | Suite 146/146. The table pins values that rot on the next commit or push; the repair makes that a stated contract obligation rather than an accident, but nothing enforces it mechanically. Whether a check should fail the commit when the table disagrees with `git` is not decided |
| M4-E124 | The EW11's own settings page puts `Gap Time` at 50 ms, which fixes the size of the error between a TCP arrival and RS485 idle, and the same session's capture carries single-frame group-off commands for lights and for heating that the protocol specification said did not exist | `capture-1788009200284.ndjson`, 54.6 min, 17,561 reads, 350,203 bytes, taken by the add-on 2026-08-29; EW11 `Serial Port Settings` screenshot from the same session | The user's own device and the user's own capture, parsed by a throwaway parser that imports no product code and knows only the length byte, the XOR over all but the last two bytes, and the `EE` terminator | The parser returned 21,094 valid frames and left 14 bytes of 350,203 unparsed, so the frame rule is confirmed without the product decoder. 20.07 % of reads carry two or more frames, and 98.6 % of inter-read gaps are at least 50 ms, both of which follow from a 50 ms serial-idle flush; a read arriving at `T` therefore describes a wire that fell silent near `T - 50`, which is the size of the confusion `M4-E111` recorded without a number. Sixteen `kind=0x02` set frames appear while the operator worked the wallpad. `F7 0B 01 19 02 40 10 02 00 B4 EE` at group address `0x10` took the light group reply from `01 01 02` at +77.2 s to `02 02 02` at +79.4 s, and a following single `f70b01190240110100b6ee` returned it to `01 02 02`. `F7 0B 01 18 02 46 10 04 00 B5 EE` took the four heating zones from `01 01 01 01` at +88.8 s to `04 04 04 04` at +92.9 s, with the same-read reply already showing all four off. Seven individual frames the product encoder emits (three light on, four heating zone on) and the gas close frame are byte-identical to what the wallpad itself sent. Reconstructing each burst as `[T - 50 - D, T - 50]` and asking how often an 11-byte write would overlap one gives 6.0 % at 30 ms after a read against 19.2 % at the current 60 ms default | Support for the frame rule and for two new group frames; conflict with `.agent/spec-device-protocol.md` §6.1 item 11 and with the comment at `bestium-eco-foret/src/protocol-debug.ts:551`, both of which assert that no batch heating command exists on the bus or in the legacy implementation | The screenshot and the capture are the user's own artifacts and carry no instruction. No product code, test or configuration changed; the specification and the plan were corrected and the falsified code comment is recorded rather than edited, because a frame watched on the bus is not yet a frame we have sent. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation, EW11 or device action occurred in this unit | Group **on** is unobserved for both devices, and individual **off** frames for lights and heating appear in neither capture. The collision curve assumes `Gap Time` has no jitter, yet 7.2 % of reconstructed bursts start before the preceding read arrived, which is physically impossible and shows the assumption is only approximate; the curve also contradicts the operator's impression that 60 ms beat 20 ms, and neither has been rejected. Why light replies cross the 50 ms threshold while heating and gas replies do not is unknown. `0x1E` `0x02` appears three times, byte-identical to the earlier capture and with no reply, so its meaning is still undecided |

The narrow band against a 1.86 s period does suggest the wallpad acts on what it polls, but on four samples that stays a hypothesis. Engaging sends `f70b01190240100200b4ee`, the group-off frame we already had; releasing sends `f70b01190240110100b6ee`, light 1 on. The other bytes of the `0x2A` reply — `19`, `1B`, `03` — never moved in this run, so nothing is known about them. **`M4-E146` later showed that reading was wrong**: byte 10 is the gas device's address and byte 11 its state, which held still here only because the valve stayed shut for the whole run. The four intervals are differences between TCP arrival times, not wire intervals: the gateway holds each frame for its 50 ms gap timer, so how much each was delayed depends on when the line next fell quiet. Only the ordering is exact, TCP preserving it. An earlier draft of this row read the 84 to 200 ms as the delay between the button acting and the command going out; measuring the reply cadence falsified that, since a polled reply cannot date the change it reports. Which room Light 1 is, and why it is the one chosen, is unknown. **Whether batch-off also turns off anything other than lights is not established**: no such command reached the bus, which is not the same as there being no other path. | M4-E136 | Heating is measured on the live bus and the silent-query gate carries over from lights unchanged: 22 armed sends, all written with no `no_gate_window`, zero corrupt bytes, and every one of the eleven in the second pass confirmed by the poll and not only by its reply | Runs `heat-observe` and `heat-zones`, 1,825 frames over 285 s with 22 armed writes, 2026-08-30 | Live bus with the add-on stopped, phase-three allowlist, every send through the gate | Direct-reply latency was 98 to 124 ms and `quietWaitedMs` was 0 on all 22, so the gate removes the wait rather than shortening it; all four silent devices opened windows (`0x34` ten times, `0x2B` five, `0x1F` four, `0x1E` three). Heating's own full reply is 74 bytes, the largest frame on this line, and a gate window colliding with it would have broken the zero-corruption record lights established; measured beforehand, no gate-opening query was followed by that poll inside 300 ms in 260 windows. **Zones 2 to 4 off rise from rule-derived to observed here**; they were the one part of the phase-three list nobody had watched. The poll cadence is 2,012 to 2,302 ms, so the confirmation window was set to 6,000. **The tool matched 20 of the 22 replies, not 22.** The two it missed are the first pass's target sends, whose replies arrived on the bus 100 ms later and sit in the frame record; the mask I wrote put `00` where the reply carries its state, so a real answer was recorded as silence. 20/22 is what the tool can claim for itself and 22/22 is what happened on the wire | Support; it clears heating for the add-on work and establishes the gate is not a light-only result | Every send was on the phase-three allowlist and through the gate. The house was read before arming and found four zones off, rooms at 24/26/27 °C against a 23 °C target, so nothing sent could call for heat; the margin was re-checked immediately before the first armed frame. The final poll matches the baseline exactly. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation or add-on capture occurred | The safety argument is a fact about August, not about the protocol: `guard.ts`'s ceiling stops the tool raising a target and cannot stop a zone turned on at the existing target from heating a room that has since cooled. Nothing was measured under actual heating demand, so whether a reply or a latency differs when the boiler fires is unknown. The group frames were not touched and the raising direction is refused by design, so neither was exercised. 22 successes put the 95 % lower bound near 87 %, not at 100 % |
| M4-E137 | Writing a heating target to a zone that was off left that zone on, both times, which the add-on does not model | Same run; zone 1 was off, received 21 °C and then 23 °C, and both replies carried state `01` with the poll agreeing, 2026-08-30 | Found by restoring badly: the first pass put the target back and left the zone on, which the poll caught | The reply is `f7120118044511150118150000000000b5ee` and `f712011804451117011817…`, byte 8 being state in both. So a restore must put the target back **and then** turn the zone off, which is the order the second pass used. `protocol-debug.ts` builds only the `0x45` frame for a `temperatureC` action and records no such effect, so changing a cold zone's target from Home Assistant will switch that zone on and leave the add-on's own state wrong until a poll corrects it | Support for the protocol, and a defect report against the add-on's heating branch | Two sends, both to zone 1, both restored. No add-on change was made | **Zone 1 only, two samples, and both on the same side of the confound.** 21 and 23 were each below the room's 24 C, so "writing a target enables the zone" and "writing a target below the room enables the zone" both fit and the run cannot separate them. That matters for the fix: they are different defects. Zones 2 to 4 were never sent a target, so that they behave alike is an assumption, and whether the value, the zone or the prior state matters is untested. What is established is that two different targets written to an off zone 1 both left it on, agreed by the direct reply and the poll. The add-on note holds under either reading, since its heating branch models no such effect at all. Whether the add-on's confirm-by-state-match actually mis-reports this, rather than merely lacking the model, has not been reproduced against the add-on itself |
| M4-E138 | `stop` over the control socket stopped the run but left the process alive, holding its control socket, until the `--seconds` timer or a signal | Closing down run `heat-zones`: `stop` returned `{"ok":true,"reason":"stopped"}` and wrote the run's `stop` and `close` records, and ten minutes later `ps` still showed the process and the socket file was still on disk, 2026-08-30 | Found by verifying a teardown claim instead of asserting it, after being asked to check rather than infer | `serveControl` routed straight to `daemon.handle`, which stops the daemon and knows nothing about the process around it; only the CLI's own `finish` calls `process.exit`, and nothing reached it. The bus connection did drop, so no accept slot on the gateway was held and the measurement was unaffected. `createControlHandler` now sits between them and asks the process to end after a stop the daemon accepted, firing after the reply exists so the caller is still answered; a refusal or a throw leaves the process alone, because ending a run over a request that changed nothing would be worse than the leak | Repair; the tool claimed an outcome it had not produced | Documentation and tool change only, no device touched | The teardown is deferred 50 ms so it cannot race the control server's write of the reply, which is a guess at a safe margin rather than a measured one; a slower host could in principle still truncate the reply. The four tests cover the handler's decision, not the exit itself, since asserting `process.exit` needs a spawned process the suite does not currently run |
| M4-E139 | Both heating group frames move all four zones at once, the never-observed on frame included, and neither draws a direct reply: the group command pulls the wallpad's own poll forward instead | Run `groups3`, 989 frames over 155 s with six armed sends, zero unparsed bytes, 2026-08-30 | Live bus with the add-on stopped, phase-four allowlist, every send through the gate; the operator asked for the group frames after the per-zone work | `f70b01180246100100b0ee` had never been seen in either capture or the legacy source and took `04040404` to `01010101`; `f70b01180246100400b5ee`, watched twice on the bus, took it back. Both confirmed by the poll. **No direct reply exists for either**: across the whole run the only `0x18` frames are the query (73) and the full poll (75), no third shape at any length, so this is not the mask error of `M4-E136`. What arrives instead is an unscheduled full poll carrying the new state, 161 ms and 162 ms after the write against a 2,050 ms median period; exactly two of the 67 poll intervals fall under a second and both follow these two sends, while the same run's four light sends produce none. A confirmation window therefore has to treat group and per-zone differently. The add-on expands all-zones-off into four per-zone frames where one frame does it | Support; it closes the group gap `M4-E136` left open and grades the on frame by our own send rather than by observation | Every send was on the phase-four allowlist and through the gate. Read before arming: four zones off, rooms 24 to 27 °C against a 23 °C target, so turning all four on could not call for heat; the four zones were on for about six seconds. The final poll matches the baseline. No push, Home Assistant update, Ingress mutation or add-on capture occurred | **One send of each frame**, which is a demonstration and not a loss rate. The pulled-forward poll rests on two samples; its discriminating power comes from both short intervals in 67 being ours, not from repetition, and whether a per-zone command does the same was not measured because its direct reply made the poll unnecessary. Writing a target to the group address is untested and off the list. Summer again: nothing was measured with heating demand present |
| M4-E140 | Sending the light group-off frame does not move `0x2A`, which is what the batch-off decode predicted and had only been assumed | Same run: 91 `0x2A` replies, byte 9 `02` in every one, while the lights went `010202` to `020202` to `010101` and back | The question was left open in the batch-off analysis, where the desynchronisation concern was withdrawn without being tested | `f70b01190240100200b4ee` is the exact frame that flows when the entrance switch engages, so if `0x2A` tracked the bus it would have engaged here. It did not, over the whole run. `M4-E134` found byte 9 changing before the light command and read the button as setting this device first with the light frame following; that reading predicts precisely this negative result, so the withdrawal is now an active falsification rather than a retraction. The consequence runs the other way too: **turning every light off over the bus leaves the wallpad's batch-off indicator released**, so the two states can diverge and anything displaying batch-off has to know that | Support for `M4-E134`, and a caveat for any add-on feature that surfaces batch-off | Observation only; no frame was sent to `0x2A`, and none exists to send | The negative covers one frame in one run. Whether the wallpad engages `0x2A` for some other reason, or on a longer horizon than 155 s, is untested, and the group-**on** frame's effect on it was not examined separately since it is not what the button sends. Nothing here says what the indicator does when the state diverges, only that it can |
| M4-E141 | All four heating zones take an individual target, and writing one switches its zone on in every zone, not only zone 1 | Run `phase5`, 1,362 frames over 214 s with eleven armed sends, zero unparsed bytes; nine target and off sends to zones 2, 3 and 4, each confirmed by its direct reply and by the poll, 2026-08-30 | Live bus with the add-on stopped, phase-five allowlist, every send through the gate; the operator asked for the target to be measured per zone | Direct replies came in 99 to 110 ms and every one of the six target writes carries state `01`, matching the two on zone 1 in `M4-E137`. **That kills the zone-dependent reading**: eight target writes across four zones all switch their zone on. Restoring is put the target back, then turn the zone off, which is what each zone's third send does. Zone 4's reply shows `1b` as its current temperature where zone 3 shows the same, so the slot ordering in the full poll and the direct reply agree | Support; it completes per-zone heating and strengthens `M4-E137` from one zone to four | Every send was on the phase-five allowlist and through the gate. All targets are 21 or 23 °C against rooms at 24 to 27 °C, so none could call for heat, and the guard's ceiling still refuses anything above 23 by value rather than by address, which a test asserts for the three newly opened zones. The final poll matches the baseline | **The confound in `M4-E137` survives.** All eight values were below the room, so "writing a target enables the zone" and "writing a target below the room enables it" still both fit. Separating them needs a target above the room, which means real heating demand and a ceiling the tool refuses; it cannot be settled this season. Two samples per zone, and only the two values 21 and 23 were tried |
| M4-E142 | The derived `0x2A` set frames do not work: both were written to the bus and ignored, which tests the absence rather than assuming it | Same run: `f70b012a024010010084ee` and `f70b012a024010020087ee` written through the gate; across the run all 111 `0x2A` replies are the single frame `f70e012a0440100019021b0385ee` and the lights never left `020202` | The operator challenged "there is no command to send" as an argument from absence, which it was, and asked for it to be tried | The candidates put this device's own sub-command `40` and address `10` into the light set-frame skeleton, with the reply's state byte as the value. In the 1.5 s after the engage the line carried only routine polling — elevator, ventilation, outlet, light, heating, entrance — and nothing to or from `0x2A`. The release reported `reply=polling` at 399 ms, which is a **false positive**: `0x2A` was already `02` and the mask was written for it, and the same record's `matchingFrameAgoMs` of 1,504 ms says a matching frame preceded the write. The design fault was mine, taking the engage's success as the premise for the release's mask; a chained test must not do that. The operator also corrected the switch's location: it is at the front door, not on the wallpad, which is why the wallpad reads this device rather than driving it | Support for `M4-E134`, and it converts an argument from absence into a tested negative | Two frames written, both ignored, nothing changed, nothing to restore. No frame was sent to any device outside the phase-five list | **This is a negative on one shape, not a proof that no set command exists.** Untried: the fourteen-byte skeleton the reply itself uses, with its `19 <state> 1B 03` tail; a sub-command other than `40`; an address other than `10`. Each is another invented frame and none was sent without asking. The contrast that gives this weight is that the heating group-on frame, derived the same way and equally unobserved, worked on its first send |
| M4-E143 | `0x2A` does have a set command, falsifying this project's own claim that it had none, and sending it reproduces the entrance switch's whole chain | Run `batchoff2`, 530 frames with zero unparsed bytes; both frames written through the gate and answered directly in 133 ms and 130 ms, 2026-08-30 | Legacy source read under the AGENTS.md clause that permits it for protocol specification, after the operator rejected "there is no command" as an argument from absence | `f70c012a0240110119009bee` engages and `f70c012a02401102190098ee` releases. The command reply is twelve bytes, `F7 0C 01 2A 04 40 11 <value> 19 <state> <XOR> EE`, a different shape from the fourteen-byte query reply, and byte 9 is the state in both. The chain after the engage is on record: the device answers at +133 ms, the wallpad's routine poll reads the new state at +1,473 ms, and the wallpad emits `f70b01190240100200b4ee` at +1,546 ms, 73 ms after that poll. That is exactly the causal model `M4-E134` built from watching the switch, with us pulling the first link. The release likewise drew light 1 on. `M4-E140` is unaffected and the two are different directions: commanding `0x2A` moves the lights, commanding the lights does not move `0x2A` | Correction; it reverses `M4-E142`'s reading and, with the operator's statement that the wallpad cannot reach the other rooms, makes this the only path to a whole-house off | Two frames written, engaged for about five seconds, then released. The release turned light 1 on and it was turned off to restore the `020202` the run found. The operator approved this specific action after being told the scope had grown from three lamps to the whole home. Nothing was copied from the legacy into the product | **Whether the other rooms actually went dark is unverified.** The wallpad reports only its own three lamps and no other light device appears on this line, so only a person in the house can say what those five seconds looked like; that is the finding's largest gap. One send of each frame. The engaged state was held five seconds, so nothing is known about a longer hold. Only the values `01` and `02` the legacy builds were tried |
| M4-E144 | A set frame need not use the address its query uses, which is why the derived batch-off candidates failed | Contrast between `M4-E142`'s ignored `f70b012a024010010084ee` and the working `f70c012a0240110119009bee` | Extracted after the fact, because the same mistake is available on every device still undecoded | The rule was already visible in the lights: `10` is the group address and `11` to `13` the individual lamps, and heating repeats it with `10` for all zones and `11` to `14` per zone. `0x2A` follows it too — queried at `10`, set at `11` — and the derivation applied the query's address to the set anyway. Two further errors rode along: the length was taken from the light frame rather than from this device's own shape, and the `19 00` payload the reply's `19 <state> 1B 03` tail implies was dropped. The general form is that a device's set frame is built from its **own** reply, not from another device's set frame | Method; it is a caution for the elevator, entrance, outlet and ventilation, none of which have been decoded | No device action; this row is an inference from two recorded runs | It is one instance generalised. Whether every device on this bus separates query and set addresses the same way is untested, and three devices with no decoded set frame remain. The rule also does not say what the extra payload bytes mean, only that this device's reply is where they came from |

The reply `f70b011b0443110303b0ee` matches the single one in `capture-1788009200284`, which was the wallpad's, so both the frame and its answer are now confirmed from two independent directions. `matchingFrameAgoMs` is `None`, so unlike the `0x2A` release in `M4-E142` this is not a poll landing in the window. **The add-on's confirm-by-state-match leans on a device answering a command it has nothing to do, and that premise is now checked on gas.** The direct-reply shape `F7 0B 01 1B 04 43 11 <value> <state> <XOR> EE` was missing from the specification and is added | Support; it clears gas reading and the closing direction without touching the valve | One frame, on the phase-seven list, through the gate. The valve was closed before and after; nothing physical changed. Opening stays refused at every phase and under `allow-all`, asserted for six values and not only `04`.
